- **[Lookahead Prediction-Guided Adaptive Visual Refocusing for Interleaved Multimodal Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02780)**  `arXiv:2611.02780`  `cs.CV`  
  _Mengzhao Wang, Yanli Ji, Peng Ye_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Interleaved multimodal chain-of-thought reasoning improves the visual reasoning capabilities of multimodal large language models by dynamically incorporating visual evidence during reasoning. However, existing methods primarily select evidence from historical context. When the reasoning focus shifts, history-guided evidence may remain focused on prior regions and fail to cover the visual information required by the next step. We refer to this lag between visual evidence and subsequent reasoning demands as Temporal Evidence Myopia. To address this issue, we propose Lookahead Prediction-guided Adaptive Visual Refocusing (LPAVR), a training-free test-time framework. LPAVR transforms Multi-Token Prediction (MTP) from a decoding acceleration mechanism into a lookahead signal for anticipating upcoming reasoning intent. During MTP decoding, the draft head generates multiple future tokens, while the target model verifies them in parallel and retains the longest prefix consistent with its decoding trajectory. This target-verified prefix contains near-future semantics, which LPAVR uses to construct lookahead-guided visual evidence and assess whether history-guided evidence covers the indicated regions. If the coverage is sufficient, the history-guided evidence is retained; otherwise, LPAVR switches to the lookahead-guided evidence. This better aligns the visual context with upcoming reasoning and improves consistency between draft predictions and target-model decoding. Experiments on multiple multimodal reasoning benchmarks show that LPAVR improves both reasoning accuracy and the acceptance rates of MTP speculative tokens, jointly enhancing visual reasoning and speculative decoding efficiency.
  </details>

- **[Unlocking the Potential of Vision Foundation Models for Multi-Task Dense Prediction via Mixture of Task Adapters](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02769)**  `arXiv:2611.02769`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yunzhi Zhuge, Xinzhuo Yu, Sitong Gong, Lu Zhang, Pingping Zhang, Huchuan Lu_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multi-task dense prediction seeks a single, coherent representation that supports simultaneously discriminative semantic recognition and regularized geometric/structural inference. For vision foundation models, pretrained semantic invariances conflict with the frequency and smoothness biases of dense objectives; joint fine-tuning and single-route adapters therefore compress competing gradients into one update channel, causing negative transfer and unstable cross-task agreement. We frame this as two-level adaptation: task-generic alignment that preserves priors, and task-aware specialization that resolves late-stage conflicts. Based on this principle, we propose D2MoE , a Mixture of Task Adapters that explicitly factorizes these roles within a frozen DINOv3 backbone. D2MoE introduces Task-Generic Low-Rank Adaptation (TG-LoRA) in attention projections throughout the network to provide a stable, shared adaptation channel, and a Task-Adaptive Mixture of Experts (TA-MoE) injected only at the final attention block of each stage to enact sparse, task-conditioned perturbations of token mixing in a single forward pass. A Semantic Prior Injector further couples multi-level task features with frozen foundation priors to reinforce semantic coherence while preserving boundary and structural fidelity. Experiments across diverse benchmarks substantiate that this structured factorization yields robust multi-task improvements with a small trainable footprint, establishing a principled and scalable route to prior-preserving, foundation-centric multi-task dense perception.
  </details>

- **[GaussFlaw: Fine-Grained Perceptual Diagnosis for 3D Gaussian Splatting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02767)**  `arXiv:2611.02767`  `cs.CV`  
  _Shaocheng Shen, Zihan Zheng, Kejin Li, Lu Liu, Xiaoyun Zhang, Qiang Hu, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) enables photorealistic novel- view synthesis with real-time rendering, yet fine-grained di- agnosis of its rendering artifacts remains challenging. These artifacts are typically spatially localized and exhibit diverse degradation patterns, making them difficult to character- ize using global quality metrics and image-level assessment benchmarks, which provide no explicit indication of where they occur or what types of artifacts they represent. To ad- dress this limitation, we introduce GaussFlaw-80K, the first large-scale dataset for fine-grained perceptual diagnosis in 3DGS. Constructed through a model-assisted human-in-the- loop pipeline, GaussFlaw-80K contains 80,049 artifact re- gions across 14,289 rendered images, each annotated with a pixel-level mask and one of eight flaw labels spanning ge- ometric, blending, and photometric degradations. These an- notations turn 3DGS artifact diagnosis into an explicit and measurable task. Building on this dataset, we propose Ge- oCritic, a geometry-guided multi-task model that integrates RGB appearance with depth-derived cues to jointly localize artifacts and classify their flaw types. Beyond evaluation, we further demonstrate the downstream benefits of fine-grained diagnosis through three complementary targeted operations: Gaussian densification, attribute correction, and outlier prun- ing. Experiments show that GeoCritic achieves an AUC-J of 0.9507 and an Acc@1 of 80.08%, while diagnosis-guided re- finement improves all reported global and crop-level metrics across diverse datasets and 3DGS backbones.
  </details>

- **[Trace4D: Grounded Spatiotemporal Reasoning via 4D State Sequences](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02759)**  `arXiv:2611.02759`  `cs.CV`  
  _Wooseok Jang, Seungryong Kim, Yan Lu, Lei Ji_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Understanding how objects and their spatial relationships evolve in 3D space over time remains challenging for vision-language models (VLMs), particularly under changing viewpoints. Recent approaches incorporate geometric information into reasoning, but explicit supervision for temporally ordered 4D state evolution and its use as intermediate evidence remains limited. We introduce \textbf{Trace4D}, which trains VLMs to predict 4D state sequences and reason over their inter-timestep changes alongside temporal visual observations. Its 4D-grounded chain-of-thought (CoT) explicitly represents 4D states, analyzes temporal visual context, and connects both as evidence for the answer. We construct Trace4D-Train from in-the-wild videos with 600K 4D perception examples and approximately 70K grounded QA examples, and train with joint supervised fine-tuning followed by geometry-aware GRPO. At inference, Trace4D predicts 4D states and reasoning directly from video without reference annotations or an external geometric model. Experiments on Trace4D-Bench and VLM4D show improved QA accuracy over the base VLMs and benefits from reasoning over 4D state changes and temporal visual observations.
  </details>

- **[Geometry-Constrained Dynamic Hypergraph Convolutional Network with Contrastive Score Refinement for Skeleton-Based Action Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02685)**  `arXiv:2611.02685`  `cs.CV`  
  _Minh Ta Tran Nhat, Yuning Ye, Yuseok Ban_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Skeleton-based action recognition is a pivotal task in computer vision; however, conventional Graph Convolutional Networks (GCNs) are often constrained by pairwise joint modeling, which fails to capture the intricate high-order interactions inherent in human movement. While recent hypergraph-based methods attempt to address this limitation, they typically rely on static structures derived from anatomical priors, lacking the flexibility to capture dynamic, action-specific joint correlations. To this end, we propose DyGCN, a Geometry-Constrained Dynamic Hypergraph Convolutional Network for skeleton-based action recognition. At the core of DyGCN is the Dynamic Hypergraph Construction Network with Contrastive Score Refinement (DyGCT) block, which comprises two novel components: the Geometry-Constrained Dynamic Hypergraph Construction (GeoHC) module and the Contrastive Score Refinement (CSR) module. Specifically, GeoHC dynamically constructs action-specific hyperedges by leveraging geometry-constrained second-order compatibility, transcending the limitations of static hyperedge structures. Furthermore, CSR explicitly refines the pairwise compatibility scores during hypergraph construction through prototype-based contrastive learning, fostering more discriminative action-specific hyperedges, with zero inference-time overhead as CSR operates only during training. Extensive experiments on NTU RGB+D, NTU RGB+D 120, and Northwestern-UCLA validate the effectiveness of DyGCN, achieving state-of-the-art performance on widely used benchmarks. The source code is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[ReinSeg: Retrieval-Guided Bounded Multimodal Refinement for 3-D Road Anomaly Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02648)**  `arXiv:2611.02648`  `cs.CV`  
  _Haifa Zhang, Yijing Wang, Zhiqiang Zuo_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  3-D road anomaly segmentation is essential for risk-aware perception in autonomous driving. Its central challenge is to rank sparsely observed hazards ahead of abundant normal road structures while preserving point-wise metric localization. LiDAR provides accurate 3-D point support, whereas surround-view imagery offers complementary semantic context for separating unexpected hazards from challenging normal structures. We propose ReinSeg, a retrieval-guided bounded multimodal refinement framework for point-wise road anomaly segmentation. Occupied LiDAR bird's-eye-view cells retrieve scene-specific context from a shared memory of eight surround-view images without supplying calibration variables to the retrieval module. A relative-norm-bounded residual returns this context to the LiDAR point features, so the frozen LIDO anomaly-scoring interface is preserved, and the refinement is optimized with in-distribution semantic labels only. On the Spotting the Unexpected (STU) benchmark, ReinSeg improves validation average precision from 27.54\% to 35.67\% and reduces the false-positive rate at 95\% anomaly recall from 34.85\% to 10.61\%, shrinking the high-recall screening set from 67.54 to 20.55 million points. Relative to REL, AP is 24.99 percentage points higher and FPR95 is 0.99 points lower. Matched interfaces and component isolation show that retrieved scene imagery and the relative-norm bound are both essential, and ReinSeg stays above LiDAR-only performance under missing, corrupted, or misaligned camera inputs while running at 21.8 frames per second. These results establish bounded retrieval of scene-specific visual evidence as an effective route to reliable 3-D road-hazard perception.
  </details>

- **[BGD-SF PolySegNet: boundary-guided dynamic selective fusion network for robust polyp segmentation in colonoscopy images](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02603)**  `arXiv:2611.02603`  `cs.CV`  
  _Nishi Kanta Paul, Shamia Maherin, Morsheda Akter, Moinul Hossain, Md Kishor Morol_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Effective polyp segmentation in colonoscopy images is crucial for early detection of colorectal cancer, a leading cause of cancer-related death worldwide. While current deep learning models perform well on traditional Dice benchmarks, border segmentation remains difficult. Most methods use boundary information only as an additional loss signal during training, which prevents the network from explicitly learning border-guided feature selection or mask refinement. This paper introduces BGD-SF PolySegNet, a boundary-guided encoder-decoder architecture that controls three phases of the segmentation pipeline: dynamic multi-scale context aggregation, selective skip-feature fusion, and final mask refinement. The architecture comprises a pretrained EfficientNet-B4 backbone and three proposed modules: Boundary-Guided Dynamic Cascaded Multi-Scale Context Aggregation (BGD-CMSCA), Boundary-Guided Selective Attention Fusion (BG-SAGF), and a Multi-Level Boundary Guidance Head (MBGH). The model is trained on 3,535 annotated images from Kvasir-SEG, BKAI-IGH NeoPolyp-Small, and PolypGen, and evaluated on four held-out benchmark datasets. On the internal test set, BGD-SF achieves a Dice of 0.887, IoU of 0.839, mask-derived Boundary F1 of 0.531, HD95 of 30.32, and ASSD of 18.10, improving Boundary F1, HD95, and ASSD by 15.7%, 27.5%, and 34.6% over the strongest CNN baselines. External validation on CVC-ClinicDB, CVC-ColonDB, ETIS-Larib, and CVC-300 yielded Dice scores of 0.891, 0.776, 0.863, and 0.885, respectively. The model runs at 35 FPS with 22.51M parameters. Wilcoxon signed-rank tests confirm statistically significant mask-derived Boundary F1 improvements over six of seven ablation variants (p < 0.001; small-to-moderate effect sizes, r = 0.215-0.434), demonstrating that integrating boundary guidance as an active architectural component measurably advances polyp boundary delineation.
  </details>

- **[SeMo3D: Unifying 3D Generation, Editing, and Completion via Selective Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02568)**  `arXiv:2611.02568`  `cs.CV`  
  _Xiaokun Sun, Jian Yang, Zhenyu Zhang_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  3D generation, editing, and completion are typically treated as regenerating the complete 3D asset under task-specific constraints, even when only a local region needs modification. This leads to task isolation, inconsistency in content that should remain unchanged, and dependence on task-specific paired data. We address these issues with a key observation: the three tasks all synthesize a designated region while keeping the rest intact---the full region for generation, the target region for editing, and the missing region for completion. We term this paradigm Selective Modeling and build SeMo3D upon it; it naturally unifies the three tasks, preserves unchanged content by construction, and enables training without paired editing or completion data, since any 3D asset can be partitioned into a selected region and its context. For training, we propose region-selective denoising, which randomly selects regions of varying sizes on standard 3D assets and applies noise and loss only to them, jointly learning all three tasks. For architecture, we propose Omni3DiT, a multi-condition 3D diffusion transformer that distinguishes regions to create from those to retain and fuses image, text, and point guidance for fine-grained control. Experiments across three benchmarks show SeMo3D surpasses state-of-the-art methods on all tasks, delivering multimodal controllability in generation and strong consistency and accuracy in editing and completion. SeMo3D further serves as a unified modeling tool for agents, enabling efficient 3D creation through natural-language interaction. Project page:this https URL.
  </details>

- **[MedHEB: Benchmarking Medical Embeddings Across Heterogeneous Clinical Evidence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02519)**  `arXiv:2611.02519`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yingshu Li, Shaoyang Zhou, Zhanyu Wang, Yunyi Liu, Xinyu Liang, Xi Zhang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Clinical evidence is inherently relational: medical images are interpreted with prior studies, cross-modal examinations, reports, and localized findings, and retrieving relevant evidence is therefore central to clinical interpretation. Recent foundation models increasingly encode these heterogeneous sources into shared embedding spaces, yet existing embedding evaluation often focuses on isolated matches, leaving unclear whether current models retrieve relevant evidence across changes in modality, appearance, and granularity. We introduce MedHEB, a unified benchmark with 107 retrieval tasks from 54 datasets, including both in-distribution and held-out-source evaluation, across three axes: evidence form (2D images, 3D volumes, text), pairing type (image--image, image--text, text--text, and modality-bridging visual matching), and relevance granularity (global, question-conditioned, region-level). Under a single ranking protocol, MedHEB varies clinically relevant evidence definitions to test whether embedding proximity remains meaningful across heterogeneous evidence, including modality-bridging visual retrieval and region-level text-to-region retrieval. Across representative models, performance is uneven across retrieval families: models strong on conventional image--text or text--text retrieval are less reliable on modality-bridging and region-level tasks. Current embedding spaces capture some clinical relationships well but do not yet provide balanced retrieval across heterogeneous evidence. We further provide MedEmb, an MLLM-based reference embedding model trained on the MedHEB ID split, as a reproducible baseline for unified 2D--3D--text medical retrieval. We release MedHEB with preprocessing scripts, evaluation code, access manifests, and MedEmb to support research on clinically meaningful medical embeddings.
  </details>

- **[CellPlex: Benchmarking Single-Cell Spatial Proteomics Prediction from H&E](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02506)**  `arXiv:2611.02506`  `cs.CV`  
  _Zijun Gao, Yijiang Chen, Xiangde Luo, Pheng-Ann Heng_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Spatial proteomics measures tens of proteins in every cell of a tissue section, but its cost confines it to small research cohorts, whereas hematoxylin and eosin (H\&E) slides exist for nearly every patient. Inferring single-cell spatial proteomics from H\&E would bring its analyses to routine pathology, and a growing number of virtual staining models pursue this goal. Each is evaluated on its own cohort, panel and pixel-level metric, and released models transfer poorly to new cohorts, leaving open which approach to build on and which proteins H\&E can reveal. We introduce CellPlex, a benchmark that poses the task at the level of the cell: seven cohorts with same-section H\&E and multiplexed immunofluorescence, from colorectal and breast cancer to lymph-node metastases, bone marrow and glioma, with 717 samples, 44.8 million cells and 149 proteins, processed by one pipeline into per-cell targets. Pathology foundation models (PFMs) are adapted with one cell-level protocol, specialist models are fine-tuned under a matched budget, and all are scored on the same cells. Adapted PFMs are the most accurate models on every cohort, ahead of the specialists even after fine-tuning, but keep less than half of their accuracy on cohorts they were not trained on. Which proteins can be predicted depends on the tissue, not on how many cells were measured for them. By fixing the cells, targets and splits on which predictions are judged, CellPlex sets a common standard for inferring costly molecular measurements from routine H\&E and shows how far H\&E can stand in for spatial proteomics. We will release the data, splits, evaluation code and baselines as a testbed for developing and comparing future pathology foundation models and virtual staining methods.
  </details>

- **[Gnomon4D: Learning Where and When to Focus for Geometric Motion-Grounded World-Action Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02474)**  `arXiv:2611.02474`  `cs.CV`  
  _Lingwei Dang, Yan Zhou, Juntong Li, Zhenhao Zhang, Yiteng Chen, Xiaoqiang Liu, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  World-action models (WAMs) use video-generation priors to jointly predict future observations and robot actions. Yet RGB futures entangle physical state changes with appearance, while frame-wise depth and point maps lack temporal correspondence. Existing 4D approaches either keep motion implicit in visual features or translate predicted traces into actions through a separate interface. We present Gnomon4D, a WAM that jointly generates future video, robot actions, and GeoDelta, a video-aligned 4D geometric motion field encoding the cross-frame image-plane displacement and depth change of embodiment and object points. By placing embodiment motion and object responses in a shared space, GeoDelta exposes their motion relations to the action expert at inference. Interaction-Salient Attention (InSAttn) uses these relations to focus spatial readout on task-relevant interaction regions, while Transition-Anchored Aggregation (TransAgg) aggregates evidence around changes in their relative motion, such as grasp and release. GeoDelta describes surface motion independently of embodiment-specific controls, providing a shared human--robot quasi-action for action-free pretraining on EgoManip-5000H followed by robot mid-training on Robot-30H. Gnomon4D leads on RoboTwin~2.0, VLABench, and RoboDojo, and outperforms the strongest real-robot baseline by 7.0 and 16.0 percentage points under in-distribution and out-of-distribution settings, respectively.
  </details>

- **[Blueprint2CAD: Benchmarking Foundation Models on Engineering Drawing-to-CAD Render Correspondence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02433)**  `arXiv:2611.02433`  `cs.CV`  
  _Alexander Valverde, Danny Xie-Li, Zhichao Wang, Marcos V. Conde_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Engineering drawings specify mechanical parts through orthographic projections. Recognizing which part a drawing depicts underlies part search, design reuse, and digitization, yet no public benchmark measures whether vision models can do it. We introduce Blueprint2CAD, a benchmark for drawing-to-render correspondence: given an orthographic line drawing and a shaded render, usually from another viewpoint, decide whether they show the same part. Its data layer, BlueCAD-30K, applies an open drafting pipeline to 29,925 Zero-to-CAD models, adding view-aligned renders, clean and dimensioned drawings from six viewpoints, and surface-type metadata. Frozen encoders retrieve the matching part from a 10,000-model gallery with up to 65% Recall@1 when viewpoints are known but 43% when they are not, and the four encoders we test lie within six points of each other: viewpoint, not backbone, is the bottleneck. Six zero-shot vision-language models reach only 0.52-0.67 verification accuracy, with model-specific Yes/No biases that chain-of-thought shifts but does not remove; a quick LoRA fine-tuning on in-domain pairs improves verification accuracy by a few points but sharpens these biases and leaves the viewpoint failure intact.
  </details>

- **[TERA: Task-Evidence Restoration for Underwater Object Detection under Bandwidth-Constrained Transmission](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02419)**  `arXiv:2611.02419`  `cs.CV`  
  _Heejin Kim, Joon-Seok Song, Eunil Park_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Underwater object detectors are typically evaluated on pre-transmission images, overlooking additional degradation from bandwidth-constrained downscaling and compression. We introduce AquaTX, a controlled benchmark built from real underwater detection datasets with seen and held-out codecs. We also propose TERA, a metadata-blind receiver-side framework combining task-guided pixel restoration and dual-state feature correction while keeping the detector frozen. Under seen compound degradation, TERA improves mean average precision (mAP50:95, averaged over IoU thresholds from 0.50 to 0.95) from 37.7 to 50.0 on DUO-TX and from 31.5 to 41.3 on RUOD-TX relative to the frozen detector without restoration. TERA also preserves clean-input performance and remains competitive with detector fine-tuning under the held-out AVIF codec. These findings support transmission-aware evaluation and task-guided restoration for underwater object detection. Code is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[GEAR: Generative Editing for Scene-Conditioned Outfit Retrieval for VR Travel](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02363)**  `arXiv:2611.02363`  `cs.CV`  
  _Gia-Huy Dinh, Thinh-Phuc Nguyen, Thanh-Hai Nguyen, Lam-Huy Nguyen-Ngoc, Hoang-Bach Nguyen-Phan, Nam-Quan Nguyen, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  In virtual environments, choosing appropriate outfits requires contextual harmony with surrounding scenes, yet bridging the visual gap between 3D ambient views and catalog garment assets remains challenging. This paper investigates scene-conditioned outfit retrieval for interactive 3D environments, where a user's egocentric view serves as the retrieval signal. We present GEAR (Generative Editing for Asset Retrieval), a framework that bridges the cross-domain gap by synthesizing intermediate visual queries rather than predicting direct compatibility. GEAR uses a vision-language model to describe suitable clothing from a captured scene, employs an instruction-guided diffusion model to render the concept onto a standardized reference character, and utilizes the cropped clothing region to retrieve existing 2D outfit assets via embedding similarity. An interactive Unity prototype demonstrates real-time scene capture, asset retrieval, and in-engine 3D avatar fitting. We benchmark GEAR against direct visual similarity, aesthetic scoring, and VLM-based reranking on a controlled dataset using automated Swiss-system pairwise judgments, where GEAR achieves competitive top-ranked performance (mean Elo@5 of 1012.75 and top-10 overlap of 0.180) alongside 0.539 pairwise agreement. Furthermore, a user study across 20 virtual travel scenes demonstrates that GEAR achieves the highest overall participant preference in both visual appeal (34.5%) and contextual suitability (34.5%).
  </details>

- **[SVD-Prune: Spectral Vision Token Pruning under Extreme Compression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02284)**  `arXiv:2611.02284`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yvon Apedo, Martyna Poreba, Michal Szczepanski, Samia Bouchafa_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Vision-Language Models (VLMs) and Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models process hundreds of visual tokens, making visual representations a major computational bottleneck. We argue that visual token pruning should be formulated not as identifying individually important tokens, but as preserving the dominant low-rank spectral structure of the visual representation. Based on this formulation, we introduce SVD-Prune, a training-free framework that leverages Singular Value Decomposition (SVD) and statistical leverage scores for globally informed token selection. SVD-Prune is architecture-agnostic and can be applied at different stages of the multimodal pipeline, including encoder-side, decoder-side, and hybrid configurations Extensive experiments across VLM and VLA benchmarks demonstrate strong robustness under extreme compression. SVD-Prune reduces the visual token. SVD-Prune cuts the visual token budget by up to 99.3%, while, at a 16-token budget, encoder-side pruning lowers inference FLOPs by 82.4% and achieves a 4.18x end-to-end speedup, including pruning overhead.
  </details>

- **[Depth Anyviewpoint? Evaluating Monocular Depth Over Viewpoint Sequences](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02203)**  `arXiv:2611.02203`  `cs.CV`  
  _Theo Rode, Rui Zhang, Ryan Aubrey, Olivia Li, Jessica Huang, Liv Chu, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Monocular depth estimation aims to predict depth maps from single RGB images (forgoing the need for stereo camera pairs) and has seen impressive leaps in performance in recent work due to the introduction of affine-invariant losses and the use of foundation model features. These monocular depth models predict affine-invariant depth, which describes the relative positions of objects in the scene instead of absolute depth. While these models demonstrate strong performance on single image benchmarks and qualitatively consistent predictions in video, their effectiveness at preserving consistent spatial structure has been less thoroughly explored. Many potential real-world applications, such as handling video streams as input, rely on coherent three dimensional consistency in scenes. Further, anchoring relative depth predictions to a metric reference for a given scene is a key challenge for applying monocular depth models to real-world situations. We develop a novel evaluation protocol for monocular depth models using video sequences with moving camera positions to test the consistency of prediction across variation in viewpoint. We demonstrate this methodology over diverse, real-world video sequences where we are able to anchor depth model output to metric depth values using structure from motion. Our work demonstrates open challenges in the development of broadly applicable monocular depth foundation models, particularly with their ability to provide temporal consistency over long time frames. We also identify challenges in the straightforward adoption of structure-from-motion as a metric grounding tool for monocular depth estimation models.
  </details>

- **[Unified Loss-Aware Density Control for 3D Gaussian Splatting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02044)**  `arXiv:2611.02044`  `cs.CV`  
  _Tae-Young Kim, Jiwoo Chung, Jae-Pil Heo_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  The efficiency and fidelity of 3D Gaussian Splatting critically depend on adaptive density control, which grows and suppresses Gaussian primitives throughout optimization. However, the standard pipeline still relies on heuristic view-space gradients, scale thresholds, and global opacity resets, rather than explicitly assessing the loss-reducing effect of each split, clone, or opacity update. This mismatch can allocate primitives to already-sufficient regions, miss under-reconstructed structures, and destabilize training through blanket opacity changes. In this work, we recast adaptive density control as a collection of local loss-reduction decisions. We derive efficient Taylor-based per-Gaussian surrogates that predict the objective change caused by three primitive-level operations: splitting, cloning, and opacity decay. This yields a unified loss-aware framework with three components. First, we extend second-order splitting to a normalized joint position-scale space, enabling children to adapt not only their locations but also their anisotropic extents. Second, we formulate duplicate cloning as a surrogate minimization problem, selecting only Gaussians whose duplicated contribution is predicted to improve reconstruction and assigning each child a principled opacity. Third, we replace global opacity reset with selective soft opacity decay that weakens harmful primitives while preserving useful ones. Across standard 3DGS benchmarks, our method produces more targeted primitive allocation and achieves improved rendering quality with comparable or smaller Gaussian budgets compared to baselines.
  </details>

- **[NAS-PO: Native Attention-Strategy Policy Optimization for Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02008)**  `arXiv:2611.02008`  `cs.CV`  
  _Ke An, Shizhe Xiang, Yue Liu, Liang Chen, Pei Fu, Shaojie Zhang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR) has become an effective post-training paradigm for vision-language models (VLMs). Standard RLVR, however, assigns identical outcome-derived credit to all generated tokens regardless of their perceptual relevance. Recent perception-aware methods estimate visual reliance through image perturbation or masking. These indirect proxies require perturbation branches, while corruption sensitivity does not necessarily identify the visual evidence used for reasoning. We instead shape model perception through native attention, an intrinsic model signal. Rather than treating attention as a post-hoc explanation, we formulate it as a reward-anchored, token-aligned routing signal that encourages attention patterns supporting visually grounded reasoning. To this end, we propose NAS-PO, a Native Attention-Strategy Policy Optimization framework. NAS-PO analyzes native attention with a visual mass policy that quantifies visual allocation and a conditional visual policy that captures spatial routing over visual tokens, making the optimization well-posed. Based on this, Native Attention-Strategy Advantage Shaping (NAS-AS) draws on the visual-mass policy to redistribute signed policy-gradient credit for visually engaged rollouts. The Adaptive Attention-Coupled (AAC) loss then aligns the visual-mass policy on all response tokens and the conditional visual policy on visually active ones, consolidating the visual-routing patterns that drive successful trajectories. Experiments demonstrate that NAS-PO substantially surpasses leading RL-tuned VLMs across 7 out-of-domain reasoning and perception benchmarks, with its effectiveness consistently validated across 8B and 32B. Visual-Routing Sensitivity (VRS) analysis further indicates that the optimized attention prioritizes visually load-bearing regions.
  </details>

- **[Vision-language classification of Torsades de Pointes risk from pseudo-coloured ECG images: do T-wave morphology and colour context help or hinder?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01921)**  `arXiv:2611.01921`  `cs.CV`  
  _Dario Labrador Alonso, Alaa Alahmadi_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Contextual prompting is widely presented as a cheap route to better performance from vision models, but the evidence comes largely from benchmarks in which an attribute named in a prompt is also present in the image, confounding the two channels. We investigate the significance of context for a semantically complex biomedical signal classification task using a domain-adapted vision-language model, BiomedCLIP. Building on our previous work that renders electrocardiogram (ECG) signals as colour-enhanced images to flag Torsades de Pointes risk using the QT-nomogram and pseudo-colouring technique, we cross four context strategies with four T-wave morphology label sets, evaluated zero-shot and with a class-balanced linear probe, under five-fold stratified cross-validation on 9,789 images. Zero-shot inference is unusable. Once trained, the colour-enhanced images outperform the colourless ones on every metric: balanced accuracy 0.903 versus 0.825, sensitivity 0.914 versus 0.827, specificity 0.892 versus 0.823, ROC-AUC 0.955 versus 0.888 and Matthews correlation coefficient (MCC) 0.437 versus 0.311. Crossing colour-in-image with colour-in-prompt separates the confounded channels: the image contributes +0.126 MCC (+0.078 balanced accuracy, +0.067 ROC-AUC) whether or not the prompt names colour, whereas naming it changes nothing under any metric. Context is two-sided: the best prompt set gains 0.008 MCC where colour is present and 0.016 where it is not, while the worst loses 0.017 and 0.013. For a signal rendered as an image, the leverage lies in how the signal is drawn rather than in how it is described - and where the endpoint is an interval measurement, prompts naming T-wave morphology describe the wrong quantity.
  </details>

- **[RIFT: Residual-Informed Flow Transfer for One-Step Generative Video Compression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01791)**  `arXiv:2611.01791`  `cs.CV`  
  _Qizhe Wang, Jiaye Fu, Feng Ye, Haocheng Tang, Zetian Song, Chuanmin Jia, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Recent advances in video generation have enabled generative video compression to reconstruct details lost during coding using pretrained spatiotemporal priors. However, a substantial gap remains between codec-induced degradation and the perturbation trajectories learned by flow-matching models. In this paper, we identify a compression-dependent directional mismatch that degradation-strength-based timestep calibration alone cannot resolve. Compressed latents remain strongly aligned across codec operating points, while their reconstruction residuals vary substantially in magnitude and direction. To bridge this gap, we propose RIFT, a Residual-Informed Flow Transfer framework for one-step generative video compression. At its core, RIFT introduces a Residual Geometry Inducer (RGI) that converts the pretrained flow prediction into a content-adaptive restoration update using decoder-visible codec cues. RGI integrates flow-timestep conditioning with a learned full-band residual-strength estimate and content-conditioned direction and gain modulation, aligning the orientation and magnitude of the update with the compression-dependent residual geometry. Extensive experiments on three public video compression benchmarks show that RIFT outperforms competing generative video compression methods. The code is publicly available on RIFT.
  </details>

- **[FinishStrong: Learning to End Well after Imperfect Start in Video Temporal Grounding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01765)**  `arXiv:2611.01765`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yuda Zou, Boxiang Zhou, Xin Zhou, Yibo Chen, Dejia Song, Xu Tang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  A poor start lowers the ceiling, but does not negate the value of finishing well. In autoregressive video temporal grounding, models systematically exhibit an endpoint performance asymmetry, predicting ends significantly worse than starts. A committed flawed start acts as an immutable physical constraint, irreversibly capping the attainable overlap as a sunk loss. While models should minimize the remaining conditional regret, standard post-training paradigms fail to teach this adaptation. Specifically, teacher-forced SFT suffers from strict prefix exposure bias, and holistic trajectory evaluation in standard GRPO inadvertently penalizes conditionally optimal ends if they follow a poor start. To address this, we introduce FinishStrong, a novel two-stage post-training framework explicitly designed to teach optimal continuations. First, Online-start SFT exposes the end prediction to the model's own generated contexts. Second, Conditional-tree GRPO restructures the sampling topology, centering end rewards exclusively within the same-start branch to mathematically isolate the conditional end quality from the inherited start penalty. FinishStrong-9B achieves a state-of-the-art 61.7\% overall IoU on TimeLens-Bench, remarkably outperforming flagship closed-source models like Gemini-3.8-Flash and Qwen3.8-Max. Crucially, when forced to continue from imperfect starts, it reduces the subsequent end prediction error by 36.5\%, proving its exceptional ability to end well after flawed starts. Code will be open.
  </details>

- **[RSI-Env: An Open-Ended Environment for Recursive Self-Improvement of Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01736)**  `arXiv:2611.01736`  `cs.CV`  
  _Wanying Qu, Zhiyuan Fan, Jiyao Liu, Jiang Zhou, Jianghan Shen, Yanwei Fu, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Recursive self-improvement (RSI) envisions agent systems that autonomously improve their own models and harnesses, with each updated system continuing to improve itself. Existing approaches typically restrict optimization objectives, editable components, or evaluation procedures, limiting open-ended exploration and continuity across generations. We introduce RSI-Env, a persistent environment for coordinated model-harness improvement, supporting autonomous training, harness modification, and retention of research state. An independent Host Controller manages services, evaluation, and versioning. Under Self, each accepted model-harness version takes over the next improvement round. We define three settings that vary goal specificity and access to benchmark feedback: Open-General, Capability-Directed, and Benchmark-Guided. Across these three settings, we compare Self with two fixed external Teachers on seven tasks, starting from Qwen3.8-27B with a minimal harness. Without benchmark feedback, Open-General Self raises GPQA Diamond accuracy from 65.2% to 73.2% and AIME 2026 accuracy from 53.3% to 56.7%. All three Capability-Directed Self runs achieve target-aligned gains. Knowledge Self demonstrates continued improvement across system updates, with SimpleQA accuracy rising from 82.0% to 85.0% and then 88.0%. Benchmark guidance and Teacher benefits vary by task. These results demonstrate RSI-Env's support for open-ended exploration and sustained recursive improvement within the studied runs, while highlighting challenges in preserving gains across capabilities.
  </details>

- **[Deep Learning for Cervical Cytology Screening: Methods, Algorithms, Challenges and Prospects](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01730)**  `arXiv:2611.01730`  `cs.CV`  
  _Jiang Yangbo, Zheng Nenggan_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Cervical cancer remains a leading cause of cancer death among women, with more than 90\% of deaths occurring in low- and middle-income countries, where cytology screening is constrained by a shortage of trained cytopathologists. Deep learning has made automated cytology screening increasingly feasible, but the literature is fragmented across cell, patch and whole-slide-image scales, task formulations and supervision regimes, hindering comparison and clinical translation. This Review synthesizes the deep learning literature for cervical cytology screening, organizing the field along three axes---data scale, functional task and supervision---and distinguishing two clinical roadmaps: whole-slide-image confidence aggregation and cell-level detection with Bethesda classification. We examine 16 publicly available or publicly documented cytology datasets alongside the CAISHI histopathology dataset, compare quantitative performance while exposing incomparable metrics and data leakage, and assess commercial systems, regulatory status, HPV-era screening pathways and low-resource deployment. Single-cell accuracy on Herlev and SIPaKMeD has plateaued, but this reflects benchmark saturation rather than clinical readiness; prospective and randomized evidence is emerging but remains limited. We consolidate the evidence into five challenge areas---data and benchmarks, architecture and label-efficient learning, trustworthy AI, clinical translation and health economics, and evaluation and reproducibility---and provide an evidence-to-agenda map and a deployment decision matrix. Progress now depends on multi-center benchmarks, externally validated label-efficient methods, calibrated and fair predictions, and pragmatic clinical trials rather than further gains on saturated single-cell benchmarks.
  </details>

- **[MMCoT$^3$: Empowering 3D Scene Understanding with 3D-Text Interleaved Multi-Modal Chain-of-Thought Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01676)**  `arXiv:2611.01676`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yongqi Shan, Yifan Wang, Yunzhi Zhuge, Lijun Wang, Huchuan Lu_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Understanding and reasoning over 3D scenes from point clouds is essential for a broad range of AI applications. Recent 3D large language models have shown promising results, yet they still face two fundamental limitations: (i) insufficient capability for grounded multi-step reasoning in complex 3D scenes, and (ii) a text-centric inference paradigm that lacks effective interaction between textual reasoning and visual evidence. As a result, these models often fail to verify intermediate inferences, disambiguate similar object instances, and avoid hallucinations. To address these limitations, we propose MMCoT$^3$, the first 3D-Text Interleaved Chain-of-Thought (CoT) framework for 3D scene understanding. The core idea is to explicitly interleave textual reasoning with task-relevant 3D evidence, enabling the model to consult scene observations throughout the reasoning process. In addition, since a textual mention in 3D scenes may correspond to multiple candidate objects, naive object-mention alignment can inject incorrect evidence and mislead subsequent reasoning. To mitigate this multi-instance ambiguity, we design an ambiguity-aware evidence aggregation mechanism that uses query-guided cross-attention to fuse candidate object tokens and inject both evidence and ambiguity cues into the reasoning process. To better equip the model with multi-step reasoning capability, we further develop PointCoT, a large-scale 3D-specific CoT instruction tuning dataset with approximately 176.2K instances spanning captioning, question answering, dialogue, and planning tasks. Extensive experiments on six benchmarks show that our method consistently improves performance and achieves state-of-the-art results, validating the effectiveness of our proposed ambiguity-aware 3D-text interleaved reasoning for 3D scene understanding.
  </details>

- **[Text-Assisted Event Stream Visual Place Recognition via Multi-Scale Spatiotemporal Optimal Transport](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01558)**  `arXiv:2611.01558`  `cs.CV`  
  _Lan Chen, Xingxing Xiong, Xiao Wang, Ziyan Zhang, Zikang Yan, Yuanhao Zheng, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Benefiting from the high dynamic range, high temporal resolution, and low energy consumption of event cameras, low-altitude economic research leveraging event cameras has become a popular topic. Several existing attempts introduce natural-language descriptions into visual place recognition (VPR) to mitigate the sparsity of structural spatial information inherent to event cameras. Nevertheless, these methods still fail to fully address two core challenges: effectively encoding event streams and semantically aligning event streams with text. In this paper, we propose a multi-scale spatial-temporal optimal transport approach, MSTOT-EPR, to address these challenges. Specifically, given event streams and scene text information, we first adopt a visual encoder and a text encoder to extract respective feature representations. We then apply optimal-transport constraints to achieve cross-modal alignment. We then design a cross-modal bottleneck fusion module to further fuse bimodal features. Furthermore, we develop multi-scale spatial optimal transport and inter-frame temporal optimal transport modules to mine high-quality spatial-temporal event features for scene recognition. Extensive experiments on the NYC-Event-VPR and EPRBench datasets fully validate the effectiveness of our proposed framework for event-based VPR. The source code of this paper will be released upon acceptance.
  </details>

- **[Label-Free Localization and Test-Time Suppression of CLIP's Typographic Circuits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01513)**  `arXiv:2611.01513`  `cs.CV`  
  _Bohan Liu, Wenqian Ye, Guangzhi Xiong, Zhenghao He, Sanchit Sinha, Aidong Zhang_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  CLIP-based Vision-Language Models have become cornerstones of modern multimodal systems. The cross-modal contrastive objective equips the CLIP vision encoder to both see objects and read text. These two abilities induce an entanglement between the encoding of lexical and semantic shapes, creating a distinctive vulnerability to typographic attacks, in which rendered text overrides an image's semantic content with its literal meaning. The black-box nature of these encoders has prevented a mechanistic interpretation of such entanglement. We introduce a head-decomposed Sparse Dictionary Learning (SDL) architecture that, by design, decomposes individual attention heads into interpretable, head-specific latents. Based on this inductive disentanglement, we re-purpose Clarity Score and propose normalized Text Attribution Score, which are both label-free, to identify attention heads that carry typographic information. As a causal validation of successful circuit separation, we perform a mechanistic intervention that reweighs attention away from the patch tokens in the identified lexical heads at test-time. Experiments show that the intervention effectively recovers object classification under typographic attack and, across five CLIP variants and four typographic benchmarks, achieves up to 46.9% improvement while incurring up to 1% accuracy loss in general image perception, outperforming prior supervised and training-free methods. Expanding on the mechanistic intervention, we propose a test-time adaptive attention head routing to further reduce the impact on performance at matched robustness, pushing beyond the Pareto frontier of current robustness-perception tradeoff.
  </details>

- **[SpecUltra: A Unified Foundation Model for Heterogeneous Spectral Images](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01396)**  `arXiv:2611.01396`  `cs.CV`  
  _Weili Kong, Baisen Liu, Xiaojun Bi, Jiaming Pei_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Spectral imagery, including hyperspectral image (HSI) and multispectral image (MSI), exhibits substantial heterogeneity in band numbers, wavelengths, spectral resolutions, hindering unified modeling and cross-sensor transfer. From the perspective of imaging physics, these heterogeneous observations can be regarded as discrete measurements of an underlying continuous spectrum acquired through different sampling processes. Motivated by this perspective, we propose SpecUltra, a unified foundation model for heterogeneous spectral images. Specifically, SpecUltra employs 3D convolutional tokenization and wavelength-aware 3D RoPE to encode relative wavelength and spatial relationships, enabling fine-grained spatial-spectral modeling within a shared coordinate system. We further construct MultiHyper-200K, a large-scale multi-sensor HSI dataset, and pretrain SpecUltra using auxiliary-token-assisted mask image modeling (MIM), where a level token indicates the radiometric product type and register tokens absorb sensor- and acquisition-dependent variations. To bridge the spectral-granularity gap between HSI and MSI, we introduce Spectral Kernel Transformation (SKT), which transfers pretrained knowledge in parameter space by constructing MSI embedding kernels from fine-grained HSI-pretrained kernel slices. This design preserves pretrained spectral priors while maintaining compatibility with the pretrained backbone. Experiments on HSI and MSI classification and segmentation benchmarks demonstrate state-of-the-art or competitive performance across disparate sensors and downstream tasks.
  </details>

- **[StreamClarify: Proactive Clarification for Grounded Streaming Video Question Answering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01263)**  `arXiv:2611.01263`  `cs.CV`  
  _Zhuchen Shao, Bo Chen, Klara Nahrstedt_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Streaming video question answering (QA) requires real-time reasoning over continuous video streams under strict latency and active-context budgets. Current streaming vision-language models (VLMs) often give confident yet ungrounded answers when queries are ambiguous or have false premises, or when relevant visual evidence is no longer in the active context. Proactive clarification can help, but must satisfy the same latency and memory constraints. We present StreamClarify, a training-free framework for grounded streaming QA that combines a pre-answer uncertainty check with hierarchical memory. The check uses the entropy of a binary pre-answer judgment as a lightweight uncertainty score to decide whether to answer directly or request clarification. To support re-answering, a temporal-pyramid KV cache maintains a bounded active visual context, while a CPU-side semantic retrieval archive provides access to historical frames. We further construct StreamClarify-QA, a diagnostic benchmark of 1,837 QA pairs from StreamBench and OVO-Bench, spanning standard, referential ambiguity, and unanswerable scenarios. Across three VLM backbones, StreamClarify improves Macro accuracy by up to 8.2 points over backbone-matched baselines. At comparable trigger rates, entropy-based selection achieves more than 18 points higher precision than random selection in identifying initially incorrect answers. Under oracle user guidance, clarification improves Macro accuracy by up to 3.5 points. We also report a controlled, oracle-assisted Qwen3-VL simulation on Ambiguous QA with generated questions and referent-only user replies, where the entropy-gated condition improves accuracy by 8.3 points.
  </details>

- **[GESTALT: Fast, Generalizable Histopathology Image Synthesis from Gene Expression with Pretrained Pathology Foundation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01141)**  `arXiv:2611.01141`  `cs.CV`  
  _Felix Ho, Alexandros Graikos, Dimitris Samaras_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Spatial transcriptomics measures gene expression while preserving where each cell sits in the tissue. The inverse, what a given transcriptomic state looks like, is less explored but equally useful: it lets researchers visualize the morphological consequence of a molecular state and interpret a spatial assay's molecular calls against real tissue structure. GeneFlow, the current state of the art on the Xenium gene expression-to-image benchmark, trains a model from scratch, incurring a high cost on every new target dataset it is applied to. We instead propose GESTALT, which reformulates gene expression-to-image synthesis as predicting a pathology foundation model's image embedding. A lightweight gene-token transformer predicts this embedding from multi-cell gene expression, and a frozen or LoRA-adapted diffusion decoder (PixCell) renders it as an image. Because this visual knowledge is reused rather than relearned, GESTALT trains in a fraction of the time. In both multi-cell and single-cell settings, our method outperforms the SoTA on perceptual metrics across all three Xenium datasets. Beyond improved image quality, GESTALT natively synthesizes at 1024 x 1024, and is evaluated in three generalization settings: zero-shot transfer across gene panels and datasets, held-out patients and disease states, and transfer across studies and tissue sources. Coupled with a statistical model of spatial expression, GESTALT also provides, to our knowledge, the first paired synthesis of cell-resolved transcriptomic fields and tissue images with explicit control over cell type, density, and composition. Code is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Grounding Across Views: Cross-View Referring Reasoning for Visual Grounding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01074)**  `arXiv:2611.01074`  `cs.CV`  
  _Hao Gan, Liuqian Wang, Shuang Li, Yufei Gao, Wentao Chao, Chong Fu, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Cross-view object geo-localization (CVOGL) aims to localize a target in a satellite image from an observation captured under another viewpoint. Existing methods typically specify the query with a click point or bounding box, marking the target region but underusing the semantic attributes and relations conveyed by natural language. Moreover, referring expressions are often viewpoint-dependent, making query-view descriptions difficult to ground directly in the satellite image. In this paper, we formulate cross-view visual grounding (CVVG), extending conventional point- or box-prompted CVOGL to instance grounding jointly guided by a referring expression and a visual exemplar. We present GAV (Grounding Across Views), a view-aware framework for selectively transferring visual and linguistic evidence across views. Its core is view-aware referring reasoning: target-centered descriptions are derived from the marked query image, checked for target-category consistency, and augmented with a direction-free role cue. Exemplar prompting, cross-view alignment, and scene-level discrimination ground this semantic evidence in a satellite instance. GAV achieves a new state of the art on MoP-UAV with 62.55% Acc@0.5. We also introduce CVOGL-Text, a benchmark for text-guided grounding from street- and drone-view queries to satellite images. GAV outperforms the click-point baseline by 7.09 and 6.79 Acc@0.25 points on the street- and drone-view splits, respectively, demonstrating robustness to larger viewpoint gaps. The code and annotations will be publicly released.
  </details>

- **[What Helps Denoising Can Hurt Memory: Rethinking History in Long Video Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00937)**  `arXiv:2611.00937`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yubin Jing, Ziyang Zhang, Meng Chen, Yuyao Li, Haofan Wang, Yichen Gong_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Autoregressive video generators produce long videos block by block, using historical context to maintain temporal consistency. After denoising, a committing pass encodes each clean block into keys and values (KV) that later blocks read, and the same clean block can be encoded into different KV depending on the history it reads. We therefore study historical context not only as a condition for denoising but also as an input to committing. Older historical context that supports denoising can harm subsequent generation when read during committing. At fixed context size and temporal positions, older historical context lowers mean clip-to-clip Subject Consistency. Across 5 generator configurations supporting longer contexts, consistency rises, plateaus, and then declines as committing context expands. Motivated by these findings, we propose Commit Forcing, a training-free rule that organizes committing into a chain. During committing, each block reads only the preceding block's stored KV, so older historical context reaches new entries only through the chain. We apply this rule to 6 pretrained generators. On 60-second VBench, Commit Forcing improves Quality and Total on all 6 generators, by up to 1.27 Quality points and 1.10 Total points. Long-range consistency gains extend to 240-second videos. In a blinded comparison on 120-second LongLive videos, Commit Forcing achieves win rates of 83.5% for subject and scene consistency and 82.9% for overall preference against the base generator, excluding ties.
  </details>

- **[ZOPGA-KD: Zeroth Order PGA Guided Data Free Knowledge Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00908)**  `arXiv:2611.00908`  `cs.CV`  
  _Tuhin Subhra Mukherjee, Tiya Rose Pulikunnel, Vaidhav Adit, Devanand Thanissery, S.H. Shabbeer Basha_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Knowledge distillation (KD) enables the transfer of learned representations from a large teacher network to a small student network. In foundational KD, the student network uses the original training data of the teacher model to accomplish this task. However, due to privacy concerns, the original training data is often not available. To overcome this reliance on original training data, several data-free knowledge distillation methods have been developed. Some of these methods operate in the white-box setting, and a few methods generate synthetic data by training a generator network. White-box methods use the teacher model's parameters to obtain a synthetic dataset. To avoid relying on teacher parameters, some methods use computationally expensive generative approaches to obtain such a dataset. There also exist certain black-box methods that generate the synthetic data without accessing teacher parameters. These methods are also computationally intensive. We propose a data-free knowledge distillation method that utilizes just the teacher's output logits and does not require access to its architecture, parameters, or gradients. We generate synthetic data via Projected Gradient Ascent (PGA) starting from smooth random noise signals. Required gradients are obtained through zeroth-order gradient estimation rather than backpropagating through the teacher's parameters. The student is then trained on these synthetically generated samples. We evaluate the proposed method on several benchmark datasets and show that the distillation performance is comparable with approaches that have access to the teacher model parameters.
  </details>

- **[Learning to Group and Order: Cross-Instance Self-Supervised RL for Vision-Centric MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00892)**  `arXiv:2611.00892`  `cs.CV`  
  _Songming Yang, Hong-Tao Yu, Xiu-Shen Wei_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Reinforcement learning (RL) post-training has become an important paradigm for improving multimodal large language models (MLLMs), especially with automatically verifiable rewards. Self-supervised visual pretext tasks provide such rewards without human annotations. However, existing methods mainly exploit within-instance structure, offering limited supervision for cross-instance discrimination and fine-grained comparison. We introduce Group-and-Order Self-Supervised Reinforcement Learning (GO-SSL), a cross-instance jigsaw framework for vision-centric MLLM post-training. Given two visual instances, GO-SSL mixes their local elements and trains the model to group them by instance of origin while recovering the order within each group, coupling instance-level comparison with spatial, temporal, or geometric structure recovery. This paired formulation turns the same unlabeled data into richer verifiable supervision through diverse pairings, shuffles, and a random-to-hard curriculum. We instantiate GO-SSL mainly on cross-image jigsaw tasks and further extend it to 3D depth and video temporal ordering. Across diverse benchmarks, GO-SSL consistently improves over the base MLLM and prior self-supervised post-training baselines, with clear gains in fine-grained perception and spatial understanding. Further evaluations across model scales and families support its generality. These results suggest that cross-instance comparison provides a useful self-supervised RL signal for improving transferable visual understanding in MLLMs. Code is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Toward Counterfactual Learning for Selective Re-Examination in Agentic Visual Search](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00848)**  `arXiv:2611.00848`  `cs.CV`  
  _Ao Chen, Shengchao Chen, Yan Luximon_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Visual search over high-resolution images requires a vision-language model (VLM) not only to determine where to inspect, but also whether another observation is warranted before committing to an answer. Existing prompting, demonstrations, and outcome-based reinforcement learning can encourage re-examination, yet do not directly supervise whether it is beneficial at a particular search state. We introduce ReSee, a benchmark of re-examination decisions comprising 3,227 visual-search trajectories and 8,827 explicit commit-or-re-examine decisions, with counterfactual adjudication that distinguishes states where additional inspection is warranted from those where commitment is preferable. Building on this formulation, we propose CARE (Counterfactual Advantage for Re-Examination), a decision-level reinforcement learning method that compares paired continuations under commit and re-examine verdicts from the same search state. Their cost-aware return difference estimates the counterfactual value of an additional observation and is assigned only to the corresponding verdict tokens, providing localized credit for whether search should continue. Experiments expose limited re-examination selectivity in existing VLMs and show that CARE significantly improves accuracy and decision selectivity over GRPO across two backbones, with higher accuracy and fewer visual requests on six unseen OOD benchmarks.
  </details>

- **[DramaVQA: Probing Detailed Understanding of Ultra-Long TV Dramas](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00769)**  `arXiv:2611.00769`  `cs.CV`  
  _Xinyue Huo, Pengfei Chen, Jiannan Ge, Kaiwen Duan, Jiacheng Shao, Yunning Cao, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Despite recent progress in long-form video understanding, current benchmarks mostly focus on short clips or hour-scale videos. This leaves a major gap in evaluating ultra-long video understanding, where videos often last tens of hours. To bridge this gap, we introduce DramaVQA, a large-scale benchmark built upon long-form TV dramas. Containing approximately 20K question-answering (QA) pairs, the scale of DramaVQA exceeds previous long-video datasets, such as Video-MME and LVBench, by an order of magnitude. Our benchmark has two main features that make it stand out from existing datasets. First, we establish a scalable, LLM-assisted annotation pipeline that uses multimodal large language models (MLLMs) to generate complex questions, followed by strict human checking to ensure high data quality. Second, DramaVQA introduces a multi-level time structure; queries are designed across different video lengths, with the hardest questions requiring overall understanding across the entire drama. Extensive evaluations show that state-of-the-art MLLMs face major challenges when tested on these very long videos, highlighting the need for agentic AI algorithms capable of managing long-term memory. The project is available atthis https URL
  </details>

- **[DADKC: Distribution-Aware Adaptive Differentiated Knowledge Consolidation for Cloth-Hybrid Lifelong Person Re-identification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00768)**  `arXiv:2611.00768`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yan Zhang, Minghao Long, Meiqi Wan, Yan Yang, Xin Yuan, Wei Wang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Lifelong Person Re-identification (LReID) aims to match the same person using sequentially collected data. In practical long-term deployments, however, the model must handle both domain shifts across cameras and scenes, and irregular clothing changes of the same identity. This gives rise to the Cloth-Hybrid Lifelong Person Re-identification (CH-LReID) task, which demands balancing cloth-relevant and cloth-irrelevant knowledge in streaming data. While existing LReID methods have explored replay-based or distillation-based anti-forgetting strategies, they typically fail to sufficiently model stage-aware domain distributions and cross-stage feature continuity under exemplar-free training. To address this problem, we propose a Distribution-Aware Adaptive Differentiated Knowledge Consolidation (DADKC) framework. The core idea is to enhance the existing Differentiated Knowledge Consolidation (DKC) paradigm with adaptive distribution modeling. To this end, three lightweight modules are designed: (1) an Anchor Pool Adapter (APA) to retrieve learnable anchor cues for refining identity features; (2) a Phase Path Mixer (PPM) to perform stage-aware expert routing for integrating domain-specific distribution paths; and (3) a Stage Bridge Alignment (SBA) module to regularize the continuity of base, anchor, and path representations between adjacent stages. Extensive experiments on the CH-LReID and LReID benchmarks show that DADKC gains 4.1%/4.5% mAP/R1 on Order-1 and 3.6%/3.4% mAP/R1 on Order-2 over the DKC baseline, and lowers weighted forgetting from 2.4%/1.7% to 1.8%/1.4%, while storing neither historical images nor per-image features. The code is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Smooth bounding box for object detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00676)**  `arXiv:2611.00676`  `cs.CV`  
  _Chao Zhou, Shenghua He, Fengchuang Xing, Han Yang, Qiangguo Yu_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Deep learning-based object detection models are highly dependent on the quality of annotated training data. However, large-scale datasets often contain inevitable label noise, with bounding box localization errors being a prevalent and impactful form in object detection. While label noise handling techniques are well-studied for image classification, their adaptation to the more complex problem of bounding box errors remains underexplored. To address this, we propose a novel Smooth Bounding Box (SBB) strategy to mitigate the adverse effects of bounding box annotation inaccuracies. Our key insight is that annotation errors are often linked to annotator factors rather than specific image content, allowing for a statistical correction approach. Based on object size distribution within datasets, we implement the SBB strategy through two methods: 1) Global Scaling Correction (GSC), which applies a uniform scaling factor to estimate and correct a global average error; and 2) Interval Scaling Correction (ISC), which categorizes objects by size into intervals and estimates a dedicated correction factor for each, enabling more precise, fine-grained calibration. For efficient optimization of multiple parameters in ISC, we design a tailored model fine-tuning strategy. Furthermore, we introduce a Normalized Residual Fluctuation (NRF) metric to quantitatively assess the smoothing effect and training stability. Extensive experiments on multiple benchmarks demonstrate that our method consistently enhances detection accuracy across various model architectures. Both GSC and ISC achieve significant performance improvements over strong baselines, including the latest YOLOv11n, without adding inference overhead. The codes are available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[SE-Mem: Semantic-Episodic Memory for Scene-Consistent Generative View Synthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00658)**  `arXiv:2611.00658`  `cs.CV`  
  _Changsong Lei, Yihan Li, Yu-Hui Wen, Huankun Sheng, Ming Li, Yeying Fan, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Generative view synthesis aims to synthesize novel-view videos from reference observations along user-defined camera trajectories. Maintaining global scene consistency throughout this process remains challenging for camera-conditioned interactive video generators. Existing approaches typically select reference frames based solely on camera poses, overlooking whether the content remains semantically relevant or even visible. To address these limitations, we draw inspiration from semantic and episodic memory in human cognition, in which semantic memory captures generalized knowledge across experiences, whereas episodic memory preserves information tied to specific past events and contexts. Motivated by the complementary roles of semantic and episodic memory, we introduce SE-Mem, a Semantic-Episodic Memory framework that organizes historical observations in a structured 3D representation and augments its elements with semantic importance scores. A two-stage memory retrieval strategy is designed to identify the most informative frames to provide contextual guidance. Taking into account both semantic significance and geometric visibility, SE-Mem preserves semantic coherence and fine-grained visual details during novel view synthesis. Extensive evaluations show that our approach improves visual quality and achieves competitive geometric consistency across multiple benchmarks.
  </details>

- **[Selecting or Solving? What Best-of-N Verification Delivers in Video Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00507)**  `arXiv:2611.00507`  `cs.CV`  
  _Martin Q. Ma, Yuxiao Qu, Ruslan Salakhutdinov, Louis-Philippe Morency, Paden Tomasello, Juan Pino_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Answering a complex question about a long video is a grounding problem: the answer turns on a few seconds of evidence, and vision-language models remain well below human accuracy at finding them. The standard way to spend extra test-time compute on this kind of video reasoning is best-of-N selection: sample several chain-of-thought answers from a fixed model, then let a second model re-watch the video and pick one. Such pipelines are reported by a single headline number, their margin over a majority vote. That number conflates two effects with opposite deployment consequences: the benefit the second model adds by selecting among candidates, and the benefit it would have supplied by solving the question itself. We separate them, measuring the Selection Increment (SI) -- selection accuracy minus the same verifier's accuracy with the candidates withheld -- alongside the margin over self-consistency, across five long-video benchmarks. The Selection Increment is largest for the weakest verifiers: a verifier that solves poorly on its own leaves the candidates the most room to help. Beating the majority vote follows the opposite profile: where the vote already sits near its ceiling no verifier we test improves on it; below that ceiling only the strongest do. On Video-Holmes, the two come apart on a single pool of candidates -- a weak verifier gains +19.3 points from the candidates yet lands 7.1 points below the vote, while the frontier verifier gains the least and beats the vote. The weakest verifier also shows a failure mode: on EgoSchema it picks by where an option sits in the list rather than by what the video shows. Read as a merit score, SI puts a verifier that fails to beat a majority vote at the top of its ranking; read as an audit, it says which of the two effects a headline number delivers. Code is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Spatial Uncertainty Analytics for Budget-Limited Inspection of Hyperspectral Scenes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00478)**  `arXiv:2611.00478`  `cs.CV`  
  _Ritesh Sharma, Yuichi Motai_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Land-cover maps classified from wide-area hyperspectral surveys are least reliable along class boundaries and in ambiguous regions, so their errors are spatially structured. We study how this structure can direct a limited verification budget: when a low-altitude UAV or a ground crew can visit only a handful of sites, where it should go depends on how classifier uncertainty is arranged across the scene and how a fixed sensor footprint tiles that arrangement. We estimate a per-pixel uncertainty surface from the disagreement of an ensemble of spectral classifiers and select sites by greedy, field-of-view-aware coverage of the uncertainty mass. On seven benchmark scenes, the highest-uncertainty decile of pixels is 2.6 to 7.9 times richer in errors than the scene as a whole, and the coverage greedy covers up to 57% of the classifier's errors with 15 sites, 1.7 to 18 times as many as uncertainty-agnostic sampling of the same labelled region. A non-overlapping selection of the footprints richest in uncertainty does equally well, so the gain comes from scoring whole footprints by uncertainty mass. The advantage persists under spatially blocked evaluation and with a spectral-spatial classifier, although disagreement then ranks errors less well. It requires restricting the surface to labelled land cover: over the whole scene, disagreement peaks on unlabelled ground, where inspections cannot be scored. The best policy for covering connected high-uncertainty regions depends on the scene and on the threshold that defines them, and Gaussian-process selection covers less uncertainty than the coverage greedy on every scene. The pipeline runs on commodity CPUs.
  </details>

- **[PRISM-Net: Dynamic Routing Network with VLM-Guided Disentangled Attribute Distillation for Explainable Fine-Grained Product Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00458)**  `arXiv:2611.00458`  `cs.CV`  
  _Chung-Ming Lo, Che-Wei Chang_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Fine-grained visual product retrieval in large-scale e-commerce multimedia systems demands discriminative representations capable of simultaneously resolving macro-level category semantics and micro-level visual attributes such as structural contour (line) and chromatic appearance (color). However, conventional backbones including convolutional neural networks (CNNs) and vision Transformers (ViTs) inherently suffer from feature entanglement and shortcut learning. To learn the micro-level visual attributes without costly manual attribute annotations, we propose phase-routed interpretable semantic mixture-of-experts network (PRISM-Net), an explainable multi-expert architecture that disentangles holistic visual features into disentangled line and color expert pathways governed by a conditional meta-router. In phase 1, we introduce an external VLM-Guided sigmoid semantic distillation mechanism with gradient-detached routing gates, utilizing zero-shot text prompts from SigLIP to supervise specialized structural and chromatic experts. In phase 2, the disentangled expert embeddings are adaptively fused with macro base features via a learned meta-router gate to calibrate category boundaries. Extensive 5-fold cross-validation across 275,454 e-commerce images (18 categories) and full-scale retrieval benchmarks (55,091 queries against a 220,363-image gallery) across SwinV2, ConvNeXt-V2, and DINOv3 demonstrate substantial gains. On SwinV2, PRISM-Net boosts full-fold product mAP@10 from 80.98% to 83.90% (+2.92%), line mAP@10 by +5.74%, and color mAP@10 by +9.12%. Furthermore, unsupervised distribution analysis of the meta-router gate reveals that both ViT and CNN backbones emergently allocate up to 6.8x higher structural line weights to rigid appliances and hardware while dedicating >96% chromatic weight to clothing.
  </details>

- **[Real-VAS: a Real-World Video Amodal Segmentation Dataset](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00430)**  `arXiv:2611.00430`  `cs.CV`  
  _Finlay G. C. Hudson, William A. P. Smith_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Amodal video object segmentation is fundamentally limited by the absence of datasets that combine real-world complexity with precise ground-truth annotations. To address this, we present Real Video Amodal Segmentation (Real-VAS), a new large-scale, zero-shot evaluation dataset built from composited real-image video. We introduce a novel data generation pipeline that enables the creation of programmatically consistent amodal ground truth for real-world footage, in which the object mask, occlusion boundary, and composited image are all derived from a single unoccluded source, without relying on human estimation or expensive 3D reconstruction. Our dataset is structured into two challenging scenarios: dynamic Occlusion, created by compositing two clips of moving objects, and a unique Container category featuring complex, physically constrained interactions. These container scenarios--On Surface Containment, Articulated Containment, and Mobile Containment--allow us to generate precise ground truth by simulating an object's motion based on its container's tracked transformation. As a result, Real-VAS provides a diverse and challenging benchmark for evaluating amodal segmentation models on realistic video with the precision of synthetic data. The dataset and our generation code will be made publicly available.
  </details>

- **[TAP-Out: Tracking Any Point in 360° via Outpainting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00406)**  `arXiv:2611.00406`  `cs.CV`  
  _Finlay G. C. Hudson, James A. D. Gardner, William A. P. Smith_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Tracking points that leave the camera's field of view (FoV) requires reasoning about unobserved scene dynamics, a fundamentally different problem from tracking visible correspondences. We present TAP-Out, an offline method that makes this out-of-view (OOV) reasoning explicit by leveraging generative video outpainting as a world-model prior. Given a narrow-FoV input video, TAP-Out outpaints a complete 360° equirectangular video, locates target objects within the panoramic scene via video object segmentation, and synthesises object-centred perspective crops on which standard in-frame trackers operate. This decoupled design composes off-the-shelf foundation models without requiring task-specific architectural modifications. On the TAPVid360 benchmark, TAP-Out achieves state-of-the-art performance, improving OOV tracking accuracy by approximately 3x over the best prior method. We demonstrate its flexibility on out-of-frame object segmentation and detection and we provide detailed diagnostic analyses across motion regimes, static versus dynamic content, and hallucination quality, characterising when and why the approach succeeds or fails.
  </details>

- **[SIEVE: Preserving Cross-Image Evidence via Depth-Aware Token Compression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00351)**  `arXiv:2611.00351`  `cs.CV`  
  _Nitish Shukla, Surgan Jandial, Nhat-Tan Bui, Arun Ross_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
